> Requires Claude Code v2.1.296 or later. `analyse` also needs `jupyter` (for `nbconvert`) in the task's environment.
62a63,65
> /poe:poe analyse poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
> /poe:poe analyse catch-up
> /poe:poe note Plotted ORF lengths per genotype in notebooks/hpv-orf-analysis/orfs.ipynb
70a74,76
> - **Analyse** runs data analyses one at a time in a Jupyter notebook; you pick each next one. See [Analysis](#analysis).
> - **Analyse catch-up** finds notebook cells poe did not write and logs them, with your confirmation.
> - **Note** logs analysis you did yourself, in your own words.
75a82,96
> ## Analysis
> 
> One task can hold both coding and analysis. Use `plan`, `split` and `execute` to build tools and `analyse` to explore data; switch by running the other mode on the same `plan.md`. Phases and analyses share `status.md` and `report.html`.
> 
> - **Start**: `/poe:poe analyse <task>`. Without a plan, it writes a short `plan.md` (question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment and how to call `jupyter`) and stops for review. Then run `/poe:poe analyse poe/<task-slug>/plan.md`.
> - **Each analysis**: a fresh worker adds cells under a heading `## NN: <question>`, runs the notebook headless (`jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace`), writes a record `poe/<task-slug>/analysis/NN-name.md` and appends an entry to `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. It proposes 2-3 next analyses; pick one or say done. Done writes `summary.md`, copies logs and builds the report, as execute does. `NN` continues across runs.
> - **Notebook rules**: cells poe writes are tagged `poe:<task-slug>/<NN>` in cell metadata. New cells go into the existing notebook; a new notebook is started only when a full re-run takes over ~5 min or the topic changes, and the record says why. Failed attempts are removed from the notebook and described in the record. Notebooks, figures and intermediates live in the project path from `plan.md`, not under `poe/`.
> - **Open notebooks**: `--inplace` rewrites the file, so save your edits before an analysis runs. The worker checks `git status` first and stops with a warning if the notebook changed after it read it.
> - **Analysis log**: `poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md` is one chronology for the whole project, across tasks, meant for writing up. Each entry gives the date, question, a one-line result, who did it (poe, user, other agent) and links.
> - **Untracked work** (analysis done outside poe, by you or another agent) shows up as untagged code cells. Every `analyse` run starts with a catch-up check (`analyse catch-up` runs it alone): for each group of adjacent untagged cells it drafts a `manual` log entry, asks you to confirm or fix it, appends it and tags the cells `poe:manual` so they are not asked about again. `note <text>` logs such work directly in your words.
> - **Well-defined analyses** can also be planned as work units in `split`/`execute`; the worker follows the same notebook rules and writes a record and log entry. Units that write the log run one at a time.
> - `report.html` shows analysis records as text; notebooks and figures are not embedded.
> 
> Record, log entry and notebook rules in full: [`skills/poe/SKILL.md`](skills/poe/SKILL.md).
>

#6 · 2026-11-20T12:03:33.121Z · tool result · Bash
# poe-analysis ## Decisionsshow the rest (9,187 more characters)
- Work shape: both. New step-loop mode for exploratory analysis (one step, record, user picks next) + existing plan/split/execute for well-defined analyses. Why: exploration must react to data; fixed analyses still benefit from batch.
- Code home: agent-run notebook. Agent adds cells to a .ipynb and executes headless; outputs stay in notebook; user opens same file. Why: matches existing Jupyter workflow, no extra tooling.
- Switching: one task, mixed. Single poe/<slug>/ holds analysis steps and coding phases; one plan, status, report. Switch = run other mode on same dir. Why: one story, nothing to link.
- Step record: step file + notebook. poe/<slug>/steps/NN-name.md per step (question, what run, result, interpretation, next step) pointing to notebook cells; notebook gets short markdown heading per step. Why: poe files drive resume/report; notebook readable alone.
- Notebook layout: agent decides per task. Start one notebook; start a new one + save intermediates when full re-runs get slow. Why: user choice; avoids forcing many files on small analyses. Plan must state a clear rule for when to split.
- Step runner: fresh worker per step. Subagent writes cells, runs, writes step file, returns short result. Why: keeps main session small; same pattern as execute.
- File home: project path fixed in plan.md (e.g. notebooks/<slug>/); step files in poe/<slug>/ link to notebooks/outputs. Why: deliverables where collaborators expect; large data out of poe/.
- Report: step files rendered as text + links to notebooks/figures; nothing embedded. Why (user): report.html tracks what the agent did, not outcomes.
- Naming: unit of analysis work = "analysis" (not "analysis step"); records in poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md. Mode name `analyse` (verb, like other modes) = my interpretation, to confirm. Why: avoids clash with report "step" (transcript event).
- Timeline: one project-wide poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md, worker appends dated entry per analysis (question, one-line result, links to record/notebook/figures, which analysis it follows). Why: months of analyses across tasks must read as one chronology for the paper. Must not assume all work is agent/poe-done (hand edits: see next).
- Hand edits: both. `note <text>` mode logs user work in own words on demand; catch-up check at start of every `analyse` run (and on demand) finds notebook cells not covered by records, drafts "manual" log entries, user confirms. Why: not all analysis is agent/poe-done; log must have no holes.
- Few files: human must read every bit of analysis. Notebooks kept to as few as organisation and runtime need; new notebook only for a stated reason. Refines "Notebook layout".
- Untracked work = any work done outside poe: by the user or by a non-poe agent (rare). Catch-up and `note` cover both.
- Confirmed by user 2026-11-20, mode name `analyse` included.

## Objective

Let one poe task hold both coding and data analysis, with every analysis recorded and one project-wide chronology usable at paper write-up.

## Scope

- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new modes `analyse`, `note`; analysis files in Files section; analysis-record format; catch-up check; notebook rules; analysis units in `split`/`execute`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: rules for an analysis unit (notebook, tagging, record, log entry).
- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` + `tests/test_poe_report.py`: render `analysis/` records.
- `README.md`: document the above.

Excluded:
- Live-kernel / Jupyter MCP execution.
- Embedding figures or notebooks in `report.html`.
- Entry status (kept / superseded / dead end) in the log.
- Where analysis runs (local vs HPC): per-task, stated in that task's plan.
- R / non-Python notebooks.

## Assumptions

- Headless execution: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`; worker reports and stops if `jupyter` missing.
- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata (`tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`); untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Analysis numbering `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.
- `analyse` loop ends when user says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.
- Next-analysis choice asked via `AskUserQuestion`, worker proposes 2–3 options.
- `resume` on a task with analyses: reads `status.md` + latest analysis record, continues loop.

## Approach

### Files

```
poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide, date order, all tasks
poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md   one record per analysis
<project path from plan.md>/     notebooks, figures, intermediates
```

- Task `plan.md` for analysis work adds: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment.

### `analyse [task | plan-path]`

1. No plan → write short `plan.md` (question, data, paths), stop for review as Plan mode. Plan exists → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` with: question for this analysis, notebook path, record path, previous record path, log path.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error and record exists; update `status.md`; ask user for next analysis; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.

### Analysis record (`analysis/NN-name.md`)

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

### Notebook rules (worker)

- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.

### Log entry (`poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`)

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

### Catch-up check

- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
- Scan notebooks under the plan's path for untagged code cells (and notebooks with no record).
- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.

### `note <text>`

- Append `manual` log entry in user's words; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.

### `split` / `execute` with analysis units

- Unit brief gains: notebook path, record path. Worker follows same notebook rules, writes record + log entry in addition to progress notes.

### Report

- Add `analysis` to `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS`; records render as text with links, in file order. No embedding.

## Acceptance criteria

- `SKILL.md` documents `analyse`, `note`, catch-up, record format, log format, notebook rules.
- `phase-worker.md` covers analysis units.
- `poe_report.py` shows `analysis/*.md` in Documents; test added; `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes.
- Dry run on a toy dataset: `analyse` ×2 → one notebook, 2 tagged sections, 2 records, 2 log entries; hand-add a cell → next `analyse` drafts a `manual` entry; `note` appends an entry; `execute` on same task leaves analyses intact and `report.html` shows both.
- Glossary terms used consistently: [GLOSSARY](../docs/GLOSSARY.md).

## Risks

- Many record files per task vs "few files" wish: records are short and agent-facing; human reads notebook + `ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. Revisit if noisy.
- Catch-up misreads hand edits to tagged cells (edits inside agent cells not detected). Accepted; `note` covers it.
- `nbconvert --inplace` rewrites notebook while user has it open → conflict. Worker checks git status / warns before executing.
- Whole-notebook re-run cost grows; ~5 min rule is a judgement.
- Log entries appended by parallel workers could interleave: analysis units that write the log run sequentially.

## Validation

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual dry run above in a scratch directory; inspect notebook, records, log, `report.html`.
======
# Glossary

**Analysis**: One unit of data-analysis work in a poe task: a question, the notebook cells run to answer it, the result, its interpretation, and the next analysis chosen. Recorded in one file per analysis.
_Avoid_: analysis step, step, finding

**Step**: One event in an agent transcript as shown in the report (a message, tool call, or tool result).
_Avoid_: using "step" for a unit of analysis work

**Untracked work**: Analysis done outside poe, by the user or by an agent not using poe. Enters the analysis log as a `manual` entry through a note or the catch-up check.
_Avoid_: hand-written work, manual work

1 --- 2 name: poe 3 description: Plan, decompose, and execute complex coding or analysis tasks in phases using fresh-context subagents and file-based progress notes. Use when the user explicitly requests staged multi-agent work, context-conscious orchestration, or continuation of an existing phase plan.show the rest (15,224 more characters)
4	argument-hint: "[plan|grill|split|execute|resume|report] [task description or plan path]"
5	disable-model-invocation: true
6	---
7	
8	# Phase-Orchestrated Work
9	
10	User request: $ARGUMENTS
11	
12	You are the **orchestrator**. Keep the parent conversation focused on decisions, dependency management, integration, and verification. Delegate bounded implementation or research to fresh-context subagents rather than accumulating their intermediate work in the parent context.
13	
14	## Output style
15	
16	Be concise. Sacrifice grammar for the sake of concision.
17	
18	- Applies to every reply and every file poe writes for the user: `plan.md`, phase briefs, `status.md`, `summary.md`.
19	- Bullets and fragments over prose.
20	- Applies to every reply, including errors, failures, and answers to questions; put long content in files.
21	- Never drop required content: acceptance criteria, risks, verification commands, exact file paths, test results, unknowns.
22	
23	## Operating modes
24	
25	- `plan <task>` (or a task description without a mode): write `plan.md` only, then stop so the user can review it. Do not split it into phases and do not edit implementation files.
26	- `grill <task | plan-path>`: interview the user on open decisions, then write or update `plan.md` (see Grill mode). May write only `poe/<slug>/plan.md`, `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md`, and `poe/docs/adr/`; never implementation files.
27	- `split [plan-path]`: split a reviewed `plan.md` into phases and work units, then stop for user approval. Do not edit implementation files.
28	- `execute [plan-path]`: execute a plan that has been split and approved. If the plan has no phase briefs yet, say so and ask the user to run `split` first.
29	- When a mode is given no path, use the unambiguous active plan in `poe/` (never `poe/docs/` or `poe/trash/`); otherwise ask for the path.
30	- `resume [plan-path]`: read `status.md` and the progress notes, compare them with the actual files, tests, and git diff, reconcile discrepancies, and continue only incomplete approved work. When it finishes or stops, write `summary.md`, copy the logs, and build the report as in Execute mode step 9.
31	- `report [plan-path]`: copy this session's logs as in the Logs section, build the report as in the Report section, and give the user the path of `report.html`. Do not edit the task's other files or any implementation files.
32	  Reply: report path + any failure, nothing else.
33	- For small, single-file or tightly coupled changes, recommend doing the work directly instead of forcing subagents. If the user still requests orchestration, comply.
34	
35	## Files
36	
37	Everything for one task lives in `poe/<short-task-slug>/`, relative to the current working directory (the directory the session was started in, even if that is not the repository root). Create `poe/` and the task directory if they do not exist (`mkdir -p`). Never overwrite the directory of a different task; pick a distinct slug. Slug `docs` is reserved.
38	
39	```
40	poe/docs/              shared across tasks, created only when first needed (written by `grill`)
41	  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
42	  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
43	poe/<short-task-slug>/
44	  plan.md              what will be built and why (written by `plan`)
45	  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units (written by `split`)
46	  status.md            state of every phase and unit, and the next action
47	  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker during execution
48	  summary.md           execution summary, overwritten at the end of each `execute`/`resume` run
49	  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
50	  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts of each session that worked on this task
51	    <session-id>.jsonl         main (orchestrator) transcript
52	    subagents/agent-*.jsonl    worker transcripts
53	    subagents/agent-*.meta.json
54	```
55	
56	## Plan mode: write `plan.md`
57	
58	1. Inspect only the relevant repository context, instructions, tests, and environment. Use read-only exploration subagents when exploration would otherwise overwhelm parent context. Also read `poe/docs/` if present.
59	2. Write `poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md` from what the user wants to do: objective, scope and exclusions, assumptions, the approach, acceptance criteria, risks, and how the result will be tested or validated. This file describes the work itself; it does not mention phases, subagents, or token budgets. Keep it short: do not restate the request, and give background only where it shapes a decision.
60	3. **Do not overengineer.** Plan the simplest approach that meets the stated requirements:
61	   - Build only what the user asked for. No speculative features, configuration options, plugin systems, or abstraction layers added "for later".
62	   - Prefer the tools, libraries, and patterns the repository already uses over introducing new ones.
63	   - The resulting code must stay readable by humans: plain control flow, descriptive names, small functions, and comments only where the reason is not obvious. Prefer a few clear lines over a clever one.
64	   - Keep the process as light as the code. Do not add steps, files, or reviews that the task does not need.
65	4. Open decisions (the user's to make, not settleable from the code or sensible defaults) → do not guess; switch to Grill mode with the task. Otherwise write the plan in one shot.
66	5. Show a short summary of the plan, state the path of `plan.md`, and stop. Point to the file; do not repeat its contents. The user reviews the file and may ask for changes; apply requested changes to `plan.md` and stop again. Do not proceed to the split until the user runs `split`. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with a `Next:` line and a fenced block holding the exact command with the full plan path, so it can be pasted into a fresh `/clear`ed session. Nothing after the block:
67	   ````
68	   Next:
69	   ```
70	   /poe:poe split poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
71	   ```
72	   ````
73	
74	## Grill mode: interview, then write `plan.md`
75	
76	1. Do Plan step 1, then read `poe/docs/` and `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/GRILLING.md`; follow GRILLING.md for questions and writes.
77	2. Argument is an existing `plan.md` → load it; grill only its assumptions and open or risky points. Otherwise → new slug and a `plan.md` holding only `## Decisions`.
78	3. Ask one question, then stop. After the answer, write as GRILLING.md says, then ask the next.
79	4. Frontier empty → ask the user to confirm shared understanding.
80	5. Confirmed → write or update the full plan (Plan steps 2–3; keep `## Decisions`; link new ADRs), then reply as in Plan step 5 (`Next:` split block).
81	6. Copy the logs (see Logs) at the end of the mode.
82	
83	## Split mode: split `plan.md` into phases and work units
84	
85	Read `plan.md` as it currently is on disk, including any edits the user made by hand. If phase briefs already exist from an earlier split and no unit has been started, replace them; if work has started, ask before changing them.
86	
87	1. Split the plan into ordered **phases** with explicit dependencies, and each phase into **work units**. One work unit is handled by one fresh subagent and must be independently verifiable.
88	2. Size each unit so that one subagent can finish it in roughly 100k tokens of context, counting the files it must read, the edits, and the test output. Split a unit that looks larger; merge units that are trivially small. Use as few phases and units as the work actually needs.
89	3. Identify which units can run in parallel because they do not modify overlapping files or share mutable state. Run dependent or overlapping units sequentially, or isolate them in separate git worktrees and integrate deliberately.
90	4. Write one brief per phase at `phases/NN-name.md`. For each unit give: goal, inputs and files to read, files it may change, explicit non-goals, exact verification commands, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. When `poe/docs/GLOSSARY.md` or relevant `poe/docs/adr/` files exist, list them in each unit's inputs.
91	5. Write `status.md` listing every phase and unit as pending.
92	6. Show the phase split, state where the files are, and ask for approval. Point to the files; do not repeat their contents. Do not start implementation in `split` mode. Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents; the `Next:` block is not counted. End the reply with the same `Next:` line and fenced block as Plan mode step 5, holding the execute command. Nothing after the block:
93	   ````
94	   Next:
95	   ```
96	   /poe:poe execute poe/<short-task-slug>/plan.md
97	   ```
98	   ````
99	
100	## Execute mode
101	
102	1. Read `plan.md`, `status.md`, and the brief of the next phase. Verify that the plan is approved and the prerequisites of the next unit are satisfied.
103	2. Dispatch a **fresh non-fork subagent** for each work unit. Prefer the `poe:phase-worker` subagent bundled with this plugin (if it is not available under that name, look for `phase-worker`); otherwise use an appropriate built-in agent capable of doing the work. Do not use a fork for routine delegation: it inherits the parent's history.
104	3. Give the worker only: the goal, the phase-brief path, necessary repository paths, interfaces and dependencies, allowed files, completion criteria, and its progress-notes path. Do not paste the parent conversation or large tool output.
105	4. The worker implements or analyzes only its assigned unit and runs the relevant tests. It writes its progress notes **as it works**, not only at the end: it updates the notes file after each meaningful step, so that the notes are current if the worker stops, is compacted, or fails.
106	5. Run independent, non-overlapping units in parallel only when safe. Never silently overwrite another agent's edits.
107	6. Check the worker's claims against the actual files, test outputs, and git diff. For significant or high-risk changes, use a separate fresh-context reviewer before marking the unit verified. Fix or reassign failed units with a new scoped brief.
108	7. Update `status.md` after every unit. Keep the parent response concise: phase, tests, blockers, and next action. Reference files rather than returning large transcripts or repeating file contents.
109	   Reply terse: ≤5 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
110	8. Move to the next approved phase after verification. Stop for genuinely ambiguous requirements, security-sensitive changes, destructive operations, deployments, or decisions beyond the approved plan.
111	9. At completion, run integration tests, review the full change set, and report what passed, what remains uncertain, and where the files were saved; point to files rather than repeating their contents. Write the same report to `summary.md` (what passed, what remains uncertain, blockers, and file locations), overwriting any earlier one; `status.md` stays the source of truth for state. Then copy the logs (see Logs) and build the report (see Report).
112	   Reply terse: ≤10 bullet fragments, no prose, don't repeat file contents.
113	
114	## Logs
115	
116	At the end of every mode (`plan`, `grill`, `split`, `execute`, `resume`, `report`), and whenever execution stops for user input, copy this session's transcripts into the task directory:
117	
118	```sh
119	dest="poe/<short-task-slug>/logs/${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}"
120	src=$(find "${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects" -name "${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}.jsonl" | head -1)
121	mkdir -p "$dest"
122	cp "$src" "$dest/"
123	[ -d "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" ] && cp -R "${src%.jsonl}/subagents" "$dest/"
124	```
125	
126	- Re-running it overwrites the earlier copy with the current, longer transcript, so repeating it is safe.
127	- The copy is a snapshot: anything after it (such as the final reply of the turn) is missing until the next copy.
128	- If the transcript cannot be found (`src` is empty or `cp` fails), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing log never blocks the task.
129	
130	## Report
131	
132	At the end of `execute` and `resume` (after `summary.md` and the log copy), and in `report` mode, build `report.html` from the task directory:
133	
134	```sh
135	python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/poe_report.py" poe/<short-task-slug>
136	```
137	
138	- The script writes `poe/<short-task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. Re-running it replaces the earlier report.
139	- If it fails (for example, `python3` is missing or the command is refused), say so in the reply and carry on. A missing report never blocks the task.
140	
141	## Progress notes
142	
143	Each worker keeps one file, `progress/NN-unit.md`, and updates it throughout its run. `resume` and any follow-on worker rely on it, so it must make sense to an agent that has seen nothing else.
144	
145	```
146	# Unit ID: status (in progress | done | partial | blocked)
147	## Done so far
148	## Files changed or produced
149	## Tests run and observed results
150	## Decisions and assumptions
151	## Next step / remaining work
152	```
153	
154	When a unit is handed to a second worker, that worker reads the existing notes and continues in the same file.
155	
156	## Context policy: about 100k tokens per agent
157	
158	- 100k is a **soft target**, not a hard limit. Never claim to know exact token usage unless a tool reports it; otherwise judge by how much has been read and produced.
159	- Hand over at a natural boundary, not at a number. When a worker nears the target, it finishes the step it is in the middle of (an edit in flight, a test run, a debugging thread that is about to resolve), then brings its progress notes up to date and returns `partial`. Going moderately over the target to finish a coherent step is correct; stopping mid-edit to respect the number is not.
160	- The orchestrator then starts a **fresh non-fork worker** for the remainder, pointed at the progress notes. Do not keep resuming a bloated agent.
161	- Prefer this file-based handover to automatic compaction. Automatic compaction is a backstop: it can fire in the middle of a step and loses detail, which is why the notes are kept current throughout.
162	- Keep the parent lean: orchestration and short summaries only; delegate large searches, code reading, and log interpretation.
163	- Avoid unnecessary parallelism, repeated repository scans, duplicate reading, and verbose inter-agent messages.
164	
165	## Constraints
166	
167	- Follow the project's `CLAUDE.md`, security practices, and user instructions.
168	- Implement what the plan says and no more. Keep code simple and readable by humans; do not add abstractions or features the plan does not call for.
169	- Preserve existing user changes; check git status before edits. Do not commit, push, merge, delete, or deploy without authorization.
170	- Never infer that a test passed without observing its result. Preserve unknowns rather than fabricating evidence.
171	- If an agent has no delegation tool, say so and execute sequentially using the same file-based phase structure.
172

- Non-goals: no change to existing numbered rules beyond what is needed; no frontmatter change.

# poe-analysis

## Decisions
- Work shape: both. New step-loop mode for exploratory analysis (one step, record, user picks next) + existing plan/split/execute for well-defined analyses. Why: exploration must react to data; fixed analyses still benefit from batch.
- Code home: agent-run notebook. Agent adds cells to a .ipynb and executes headless; outputs stay in notebook; user opens same file. Why: matches existing Jupyter workflow, no extra tooling.
- Switching: one task, mixed. Single poe/<slug>/ holds analysis steps and coding phases; one plan, status, report. Switch = run other mode on same dir. Why: one story, nothing to link.
- Step record: step file + notebook. poe/<slug>/steps/NN-name.md per step (question, what run, result, interpretation, next step) pointing to notebook cells; notebook gets short markdown heading per step. Why: poe files drive resume/report; notebook readable alone.
- Notebook layout: agent decides per task. Start one notebook; start a new one + save intermediates when full re-runs get slow. Why: user choice; avoids forcing many files on small analyses. Plan must state a clear rule for when to split.
- Step runner: fresh worker per step. Subagent writes cells, runs, writes step file, returns short result. Why: keeps main session small; same pattern as execute.
- File home: project path fixed in plan.md (e.g. notebooks/<slug>/); step files in poe/<slug>/ link to notebooks/outputs. Why: deliverables where collaborators expect; large data out of poe/.
- Report: step files rendered as text + links to notebooks/figures; nothing embedded. Why (user): report.html tracks what the agent did, not outcomes.
- Naming: unit of analysis work = "analysis" (not "analysis step"); records in poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md. Mode name `analyse` (verb, like other modes) = my interpretation, to confirm. Why: avoids clash with report "step" (transcript event).
- Timeline: one project-wide poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md, worker appends dated entry per analysis (question, one-line result, links to record/notebook/figures, which analysis it follows). Why: months of analyses across tasks must read as one chronology for the paper. Must not assume all work is agent/poe-done (hand edits: see next).
- Hand edits: both. `note <text>` mode logs user work in own words on demand; catch-up check at start of every `analyse` run (and on demand) finds notebook cells not covered by records, drafts "manual" log entries, user confirms. Why: not all analysis is agent/poe-done; log must have no holes.
- Few files: human must read every bit of analysis. Notebooks kept to as few as organisation and runtime need; new notebook only for a stated reason. Refines "Notebook layout".
- Untracked work = any work done outside poe: by the user or by a non-poe agent (rare). Catch-up and `note` cover both.
- Confirmed by user 2026-11-20, mode name `analyse` included.

## Objective

Let one poe task hold both coding and data analysis, with every analysis recorded and one project-wide chronology usable at paper write-up.

## Scope

- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new modes `analyse`, `note`; analysis files in Files section; analysis-record format; catch-up check; notebook rules; analysis units in `split`/`execute`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: rules for an analysis unit (notebook, tagging, record, log entry).
- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` + `tests/test_poe_report.py`: render `analysis/` records.
- `README.md`: document the above.

Excluded:
- Live-kernel / Jupyter MCP execution.
- Embedding figures or notebooks in `report.html`.
- Entry status (kept / superseded / dead end) in the log.
- Where analysis runs (local vs HPC): per-task, stated in that task's plan.
- R / non-Python notebooks.

## Assumptions

- Headless execution: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`; worker reports and stops if `jupyter` missing.
- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata (`tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`); untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Analysis numbering `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.
- `analyse` loop ends when user says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.
- Next-analysis choice asked via `AskUserQuestion`, worker proposes 2–3 options.
- `resume` on a task with analyses: reads `status.md` + latest analysis record, continues loop.

## Approach

### Files

```
poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide, date order, all tasks
poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md   one record per analysis
<project path from plan.md>/     notebooks, figures, intermediates
```

- Task `plan.md` for analysis work adds: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment.

### `analyse [task | plan-path]`

1. No plan → write short `plan.md` (question, data, paths), stop for review as Plan mode. Plan exists → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` with: question for this analysis, notebook path, record path, previous record path, log path.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error and record exists; update `status.md`; ask user for next analysis; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.

### Analysis record (`analysis/NN-name.md`)

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

### Notebook rules (worker)

- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.

### Log entry (`poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`)

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

### Catch-up check

- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
- Scan notebooks under the plan's path for untagged code cells (and notebooks with no record).
- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.

### `note <text>`

- Append `manual` log entry in user's words; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.

### `split` / `execute` with analysis units

- Unit brief gains: notebook path, record path. Worker follows same notebook rules, writes record + log entry in addition to progress notes.

### Report

- Add `analysis` to `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS`; records render as text with links, in file order. No embedding.

## Acceptance criteria

- `SKILL.md` documents `analyse`, `note`, catch-up, record format, log format, notebook rules.
- `phase-worker.md` covers analysis units.
- `poe_report.py` shows `analysis/*.md` in Documents; test added; `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes.
- Dry run on a toy dataset: `analyse` ×2 → one notebook, 2 tagged sections, 2 records, 2 log entries; hand-add a cell → next `analyse` drafts a `manual` entry; `note` appends an entry; `execute` on same task leaves analyses intact and `report.html` shows both.
- Glossary terms used consistently: [GLOSSARY](../docs/GLOSSARY.md).

## Risks

- Many record files per task vs "few files" wish: records are short and agent-facing; human reads notebook + `ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. Revisit if noisy.
- Catch-up misreads hand edits to tagged cells (edits inside agent cells not detected). Accepted; `note` covers it.
- `nbconvert --inplace` rewrites notebook while user has it open → conflict. Worker checks git status / warns before executing.
- Whole-notebook re-run cost grows; ~5 min rule is a judgement.
- Log entries appended by parallel workers could interleave: analysis units that write the log run sequentially.

## Validation

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual dry run above in a scratch directory; inspect notebook, records, log, `report.html`.
----
# Glossary

**Analysis**: One unit of data-analysis work in a poe task: a question, the notebook cells run to answer it, the result, its interpretation, and the next analysis chosen. Recorded in one file per analysis.
_Avoid_: analysis step, step, finding

**Step**: One event in an agent transcript as shown in the report (a message, tool call, or tool result).
_Avoid_: using "step" for a unit of analysis work

**Untracked work**: Analysis done outside poe, by the user or by an agent not using poe. Enters the analysis log as a `manual` entry through a note or the catch-up check.
_Avoid_: hand-written work, manual work

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build a step-by-step report for a poe task directory.
show the rest (6,857 more characters)
Usage: python3 poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>

Three parts: Load reads a task's documents and Claude Code transcripts from
poe/<task-slug>/, Timeline turns them into plain dataclasses, and Render
writes them as one self-contained poe/<task-slug>/report.html (inline CSS,
no JavaScript, no external resources). Python 3 standard library only.

The transcript format is undocumented, so every field is read defensively.
Lines that are not valid JSON, records of unknown type and records without
the expected fields are skipped and counted, never fatal.
"""

import argparse
import base64
import html
import json
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Optional, Set, Tuple

# Record types Claude Code writes for its own bookkeeping. They are ignored
# without being counted as skipped; any other unknown type is counted.
INTERNAL_RECORD_TYPES = {
    "attachment",
    "api-request",
    "api-request-blob",
    "api-request-shape",
    "mode",
    "session-owner",
    "atis-latch",
    "last-prompt",
    "ai-title",
    "permission-mode",
    "tools-used",
    "queue-operation",
}
INTERNAL_RECORD_PREFIXES = ("file-history-",)

TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES = ["plan.md", "status.md", "summary.md"]
TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS = ["phases", "progress"]

# Only the start of a thinking block's signature is decoded (signatures can be
# several KB). Must be a multiple of 4 so the cut prefix is valid base64.
SIGNATURE_PREFIX_CHARS = 64


@dataclass
class Step:
    index: int
    timestamp: Optional[str]
    kind: str  # user, assistant, thinking, narration, tool_call, tool_result, system
    text: str
    anchor: str
    tool_name: Optional[str] = None
    tool_use_id: Optional[str] = None
    is_error: bool = False
    is_denied: bool = False
    result: Optional["Step"] = None
    worker: Optional["Transcript"] = None


@dataclass
class Transcript:
    name: str
    actor: str
    agent_type: Optional[str] = None
    description: Optional[str] = None
    steps: List[Step] = field(default_factory=list)
    models: Set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
    input_tokens: int = 0
    output_tokens: int = 0
    cache_read_tokens: int = 0
    cache_creation_tokens: int = 0
    tool_calls: int = 0
    errors: int = 0
    thinking_blocks: int = 0  # narration not included
    thinking_recorded: int = 0  # thinking blocks with text
    first_timestamp: Optional[str] = None
    last_timestamp: Optional[str] = None
    cost_usd: Optional[float] = None
    skipped: int = 0
    parent_step: Optional[Step] = None


@dataclass
class Session:
    session_id: str
    main: Transcript
    workers: List[Transcript] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class Task:
    slug: str
    task_dir: Path
    documents: List[Tuple[str, str]] = field(default_factory=list)
    sessions: List[Session] = field(default_factory=list)
    skipped: int = 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- documents


def read_text(path: Path) -> str:
    return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")


def load_documents(task_dir: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
    documents = []
    for name in TASK_DOCUMENT_NAMES:
        path = task_dir / name
        if path.is_file():
            documents.append((name, read_text(path)))
    for folder in TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS:
        for path in sorted((task_dir / folder).glob("*.md")):
            documents.append((f"{folder}/{path.name}", read_text(path)))
    return documents


# -------------------------------------------------------------- transcripts


def read_json_lines(path: Path) -> Tuple[List[dict], int]:
    """Return the JSON object records of a .jsonl file and the count of bad lines."""
    records = []

class LoadTaskWithoutLogsTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_task_without_logs_has_no_sessions(self):
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp:
            task_dir = Path(temp) / "no-logs"
            task_dir.mkdir()
            (task_dir / "plan.md").write_text("# Plan\n", encoding="utf-8")
            task = poe_report.load_task(task_dir)
        self.assertEqual(task.sessions, [])
        self.assertEqual(task.skipped, 0)
        self.assertEqual(task.documents, [("plan.md", "# Plan\n")])

    def test_missing_task_dir_raises(self):
        with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
            poe_report.load_task(FIXTURE_TASK / "does-not-exist")


def run_main(argv):
    """Run poe_report.main and return (exit code, stdout, stderr)."""
    stdout, stderr = io.StringIO(), io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(stdout), contextlib.redirect_stderr(stderr):
        code = poe_report.main(argv)
    return code, stdout.getvalue(), stderr.getvalue()


def section(report, section_id):
    """The HTML of the <section> with the given id (sections are not nested)."""
    start = report.index(f'<section id="{section_id}"')
    return report[start:report.index("</section>", start)]


def step_html(report, anchor):
    """The HTML of one rendered step, up to the start of the next one."""
    start = report.index(f'id="{anchor}"')
    start = report.rindex("<div", 0, start)
    end = report.find('<div class="step ', start + 1)
    return report[start:end if end != -1 else len(report)]

        cls.ids = set(re.findall(r'id="([^"]+)"', cls.report))

- Verify: `grep -n "analyse\|note\|ANALYSIS_LOG" README.md` → present; `git diff README.md` → earlier edits intact; README claims match SKILL.md.
- Done when: a new user could start an analysis task from README alone.
- Progress notes: `poe/poe-analysis/progress/02a-readme.md`

## Unit 2b: dry run

- Read: `skills/poe/SKILL.md`, `agents/phase-worker.md`.

  5. Add a dummy `phases/01-x.md` + `progress/01a-x.md`; run `python3 /Users/sbandyopadhyay/projects/SCRIPTS/custom_claude_plugins/poe/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/toy` → `report.html` contains both analysis records and phase docs.
- Non-goals: no real data; no changes to implementation files; no test of the interactive `AskUserQuestion` loop (cannot be run by a worker: state as untested).
- Verify (print outputs into notes):
  - `python3 -c "import json;nb=json.load(open('notebooks/toy/toy.ipynb'));print([c['metadata'].get('tags') for c in nb['cells']])"`

#5 · 2026-11-20T12:06:21.032Z · tool result · Bash
# poe: phase orchestrator for Claude Code

A Claude Code plugin with one skill (`poe`) and one subagent (`phase-worker`). It plans a task, splits it into work units sized for about 100k tokens each, and runs each unit in a fresh-context subagent that keeps file-based progress notes.show the rest (21,835 more characters)
Requires Claude Code v2.1.296 or later.

## Installation

This repository is both the plugin and its own marketplace. Add the marketplace, then install the plugin:

```sh
claude plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe
claude plugin install poe@poe-tools
```

The same commands work inside a session as `/plugin marketplace add sreejancodes/poe` and `/plugin install poe@poe-tools`. Then run `/reload-plugins` or start a new session.

By default the plugin is installed at **user** scope, so it is available in every project. Add `--scope project` to enable it only for the current repository, recorded in `.claude/settings.json`.

### Update and uninstall

Third-party marketplaces do not update automatically. To get the latest version:

```sh
claude plugin marketplace update poe-tools
claude plugin update poe@poe-tools
```

To remove it:

```sh
claude plugin uninstall poe@poe-tools
claude plugin marketplace remove poe-tools
```

### Offering it to everyone on a project

Commit this to the project's `.claude/settings.json`:

```json
{
  "extraKnownMarketplaces": {
    "poe-tools": { "source": { "source": "github", "repo": "sreejancodes/poe" } }
  },
  "enabledPlugins": { "poe@poe-tools": true }
}
```

Once a collaborator trusts the folder, the marketplace is registered for them. They still need to run `claude plugin install poe@poe-tools --scope project` once.

## Usage

Plugin skills are namespaced, so the command is `/poe:poe`:

```text
/poe:poe plan Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
/poe:poe grill Build an HPV ORF analysis pipeline with tests and reproducible outputs
/poe:poe grill poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe split poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe execute poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe resume poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
/poe:poe report poe/hpv-orf-analysis/plan.md
```

- **Plan** writes `plan.md` from what you asked for, keeping the approach simple and the code human-readable, then stops. Review the file, edit it or ask for changes, and repeat until you are happy with it. If it finds open decisions that only you can make, it switches to grill instead of guessing.
- **Grill** interviews you before planning. It asks one question at a time in a dialog: pick an option (the recommended one comes first), or type your own answer under Other. `grill <task>` starts from scratch and writes `plan.md` once you confirm; `grill <plan-path>` questions an existing plan's assumptions and open points and updates it in place. Decisions are added to `plan.md` under `## Decisions` after every answer; project terms and hard-to-reverse decisions go to the shared `poe/docs/`.
- **Split** takes the reviewed `plan.md` and splits it into phases and work units, each sized for one subagent at roughly 100k tokens. It then stops for approval.
- **Execute** hands each work unit to a fresh `poe:phase-worker` subagent, which writes progress notes as it works. The orchestrator verifies each unit before moving on.
- **Resume** uses the status file and progress notes to continue safely.
- **Report** copies the session's logs and (re)builds `report.html`, a step-by-step view of what the orchestrator and each worker did. Execute and resume also build it automatically at the end, after `summary.md` and the log copy; if the build fails, the reply says so and the task carries on.

Use `plan` when the task is clear. Use `grill` when you want to be interviewed, or to challenge a draft plan.

Output is deliberately terse: replies point to files instead of repeating them, and plan and split replies end with the next command to paste.

## Files

Everything for one task is written under `poe/<task-slug>/` in the directory where Claude Code was started. `poe/` is created if it does not exist. `poe/docs/` is shared across tasks, so `docs` is not used as a task slug.

```
poe/docs/              shared across tasks, written by grill, created when first needed
  GLOSSARY.md          project terms
  adr/NNNN-slug.md     architecture decision records
poe/<task-slug>/
  plan.md              what will be built and why
  phases/NN-name.md    one brief per phase, listing its work units
  status.md            state of every phase and unit
  progress/NN-unit.md  running notes written by each worker
  summary.md           execution summary, written at the end of execute/resume
  report.html          step-by-step report built from the files above and logs/
  logs/<session-id>/   copies of the Claude Code transcripts (.jsonl) of each session, including worker subagents
```

The copied logs contain the full conversation, including file contents and command output, and so does `report.html`. Normally add `poe/*/logs/` and `poe/*/report.html` to `.gitignore`. Do not ignore all of `poe/`, so that `poe/docs/` stays in git.

To build the report by hand, without a Claude Code session (needs Python 3.9 or later, standard library only):

```sh
python3 <plugin>/skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py poe/<task-slug>
```

`<plugin>` is this repository, or the installed plugin directory. The script writes `poe/<task-slug>/report.html` and prints its path. It is a single HTML file with no external resources, so it opens offline.

Tasks created by older versions in `plans/` can be moved with `mv plans/<slug> poe/<slug>`.

## Thinking text in the report

The report can only show thinking that Claude Code recorded in its transcripts. By default it records none, so each thinking step reads "thinking text not recorded". The overview and each transcript's totals show "thinking recorded: N of M blocks", so you can see whether it was on for a run.

To record it:

- Interactive sessions: set `"showThinkingSummaries": true` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (all projects) or in the project's `.claude/settings.json`. This also shows the summaries in the terminal. Workers are expected to inherit the setting from the session that starts them.
- Headless `claude -p` ignores that setting. Pass the flag instead: `claude -p --thinking-display summarized "..."`.

The recorded text is a summary of the reasoning, not the raw reasoning. Sessions that ran without it cannot be filled in later.

Steps labelled "narration" are short progress updates Claude Code writes alongside thinking; they are shown as they are and not counted as thinking.

## Context settings

The 100k figure is a soft target, not a hard cap. A worker nearing it finishes the step it is in the middle of, updates its progress notes, and returns. The orchestrator then starts a fresh worker from those notes.

`phase-worker` sets `autoCompactWindow: 150000`. This is a backstop above the target, so that automatic compaction does not interrupt a step at exactly 100k. It does not cap cumulative usage. Older Claude Code versions ignore the setting without warning.

The `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` environment variable takes precedence over a subagent's `autoCompactWindow`. Setting it to 100000 would make workers compact at 100k regardless, so leave it unset or set it above the target.

## Repository layout

```
.claude-plugin/plugin.json        plugin manifest
.claude-plugin/marketplace.json   marketplace listing this repo as the plugin source
skills/poe/SKILL.md               the /poe:poe orchestrator skill
skills/poe/GRILLING.md            the interview method used by grill
skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py  builds report.html for a task
agents/phase-worker.md            the worker subagent
```

To try local changes without installing: `claude --plugin-dir /path/to/this/repo`.

# Glossary

**Analysis**: One unit of data-analysis work in a poe task: a question, the notebook cells run to answer it, the result, its interpretation, and the next analysis chosen. Recorded in one file per analysis.
_Avoid_: analysis step, step, finding

**Step**: One event in an agent transcript as shown in the report (a message, tool call, or tool result).
_Avoid_: using "step" for a unit of analysis work

**Untracked work**: Analysis done outside poe, by the user or by an agent not using poe. Enters the analysis log as a `manual` entry through a note or the catch-up check.
_Avoid_: hand-written work, manual work
====
# poe-analysis

## Decisions
- Work shape: both. New step-loop mode for exploratory analysis (one step, record, user picks next) + existing plan/split/execute for well-defined analyses. Why: exploration must react to data; fixed analyses still benefit from batch.
- Code home: agent-run notebook. Agent adds cells to a .ipynb and executes headless; outputs stay in notebook; user opens same file. Why: matches existing Jupyter workflow, no extra tooling.
- Switching: one task, mixed. Single poe/<slug>/ holds analysis steps and coding phases; one plan, status, report. Switch = run other mode on same dir. Why: one story, nothing to link.
- Step record: step file + notebook. poe/<slug>/steps/NN-name.md per step (question, what run, result, interpretation, next step) pointing to notebook cells; notebook gets short markdown heading per step. Why: poe files drive resume/report; notebook readable alone.
- Notebook layout: agent decides per task. Start one notebook; start a new one + save intermediates when full re-runs get slow. Why: user choice; avoids forcing many files on small analyses. Plan must state a clear rule for when to split.
- Step runner: fresh worker per step. Subagent writes cells, runs, writes step file, returns short result. Why: keeps main session small; same pattern as execute.
- File home: project path fixed in plan.md (e.g. notebooks/<slug>/); step files in poe/<slug>/ link to notebooks/outputs. Why: deliverables where collaborators expect; large data out of poe/.
- Report: step files rendered as text + links to notebooks/figures; nothing embedded. Why (user): report.html tracks what the agent did, not outcomes.
- Naming: unit of analysis work = "analysis" (not "analysis step"); records in poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md. Mode name `analyse` (verb, like other modes) = my interpretation, to confirm. Why: avoids clash with report "step" (transcript event).
- Timeline: one project-wide poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md, worker appends dated entry per analysis (question, one-line result, links to record/notebook/figures, which analysis it follows). Why: months of analyses across tasks must read as one chronology for the paper. Must not assume all work is agent/poe-done (hand edits: see next).
- Hand edits: both. `note <text>` mode logs user work in own words on demand; catch-up check at start of every `analyse` run (and on demand) finds notebook cells not covered by records, drafts "manual" log entries, user confirms. Why: not all analysis is agent/poe-done; log must have no holes.
- Few files: human must read every bit of analysis. Notebooks kept to as few as organisation and runtime need; new notebook only for a stated reason. Refines "Notebook layout".
- Untracked work = any work done outside poe: by the user or by a non-poe agent (rare). Catch-up and `note` cover both.
- Confirmed by user 2026-11-20, mode name `analyse` included.

## Objective

Let one poe task hold both coding and data analysis, with every analysis recorded and one project-wide chronology usable at paper write-up.

## Scope

- `skills/poe/SKILL.md`: new modes `analyse`, `note`; analysis files in Files section; analysis-record format; catch-up check; notebook rules; analysis units in `split`/`execute`.
- `agents/phase-worker.md`: rules for an analysis unit (notebook, tagging, record, log entry).
- `skills/poe/scripts/poe_report.py` + `tests/test_poe_report.py`: render `analysis/` records.
- `README.md`: document the above.

Excluded:
- Live-kernel / Jupyter MCP execution.
- Embedding figures or notebooks in `report.html`.
- Entry status (kept / superseded / dead end) in the log.
- Where analysis runs (local vs HPC): per-task, stated in that task's plan.
- R / non-Python notebooks.

## Assumptions

- Headless execution: `jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace <nb>`; worker reports and stops if `jupyter` missing.
- Agent-written cells tagged in cell metadata (`tags: ["poe:<slug>/<NN>"]`); untagged code cells = untracked work.
- Analysis numbering `NN` continues across `analyse` runs within a task.
- `analyse` loop ends when user says done → `summary.md`, logs, report as in Execute step 9.
- Next-analysis choice asked via `AskUserQuestion`, worker proposes 2–3 options.
- `resume` on a task with analyses: reads `status.md` + latest analysis record, continues loop.

## Approach

### Files

```
poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md      project-wide, date order, all tasks
poe/<slug>/analysis/NN-name.md   one record per analysis
<project path from plan.md>/     notebooks, figures, intermediates
```

- Task `plan.md` for analysis work adds: question, data paths, notebook/output path, environment.

### `analyse [task | plan-path]`

1. No plan → write short `plan.md` (question, data, paths), stop for review as Plan mode. Plan exists → continue.
2. Catch-up check (below).
3. Dispatch fresh `phase-worker` with: question for this analysis, notebook path, record path, previous record path, log path.
4. Worker: add tagged cells under a markdown heading `## NN: <question>`, execute, write record, append log entry, return ≤5 bullets + 2–3 proposed next analyses.
5. Orchestrator: verify notebook executed without error and record exists; update `status.md`; ask user for next analysis; repeat from 3.
6. Need a tool → `plan`/`split`/`execute` on same task dir; phases and analyses share `status.md`. Back → `analyse` again.

### Analysis record (`analysis/NN-name.md`)

```
# NN: <question>: status (done | failed | abandoned)
## Follows from
## What was run        notebook path + heading, inputs
## Result              numbers, figure/table paths
## Interpretation
## Caveats
## Next                options proposed; option chosen
```

### Notebook rules (worker)

- Default: append to the task's existing notebook. Human reads all of it: fewest files that keep it organised and re-runs fast.
- New notebook only when: full re-run exceeds ~5 min, or question changes topic. Then save intermediates the next notebook loads; state the reason in the record.
- No dead cells: failed attempts removed from notebook, described in record.

### Log entry (`poe/docs/ANALYSIS_LOG.md`)

```
## YYYY-MM-DD: <question>  [<slug>/NN | manual]
- Result: one line
- By: poe | user | other agent
- Follows: <slug>/NN
- Links: record, notebook#heading, figures
```

### Catch-up check

- Runs at start of every `analyse`; also `analyse catch-up` alone.
- Scan notebooks under the plan's path for untagged code cells (and notebooks with no record).
- Per group of adjacent untagged cells: draft `manual` log entry from code + outputs, ask user to confirm/fix wording, append. Tag cells `poe:manual` so they are not re-asked.

### `note <text>`

- Append `manual` log entry in user's words; link notebook if named or unambiguous; tag matching untagged cells `poe:manual`. No worker. Reply: entry path only.

### `split` / `execute` with analysis units

- Unit brief gains: notebook path, record path. Worker follows same notebook rules, writes record + log entry in addition to progress notes.

### Report

- Add `analysis` to `TASK_DOCUMENT_FOLDERS`; records render as text with links, in file order. No embedding.

## Acceptance criteria

- `SKILL.md` documents `analyse`, `note`, catch-up, record format, log format, notebook rules.
- `phase-worker.md` covers analysis units.
- `poe_report.py` shows `analysis/*.md` in Documents; test added; `python3 -m unittest discover tests` passes.
- Dry run on a toy dataset: `analyse` ×2 → one notebook, 2 tagged sections, 2 records, 2 log entries; hand-add a cell → next `analyse` drafts a `manual` entry; `note` appends an entry; `execute` on same task leaves analyses intact and `report.html` shows both.
- Glossary terms used consistently: [GLOSSARY](../docs/GLOSSARY.md).

## Risks

- Many record files per task vs "few files" wish: records are short and agent-facing; human reads notebook + `ANALYSIS_LOG.md`. Revisit if noisy.
- Catch-up misreads hand edits to tagged cells (edits inside agent cells not detected). Accepted; `note` covers it.
- `nbconvert --inplace` rewrites notebook while user has it open → conflict. Worker checks git status / warns before executing.
- Whole-notebook re-run cost grows; ~5 min rule is a judgement.
- Log entries appended by parallel workers could interleave: analysis units that write the log run sequentially.

## Validation

- `python3 -m unittest discover tests`
- Manual dry run above in a scratch directory; inspect notebook, records, log, `report.html`.
